{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DVSe5F9LPxoA4n4aVUiuxm","type":"tool_result","content":"480\t        return null;\n481\t      }\n482\t\n483\t      const messageRows: unknown = await queryRunner.query(\n484\t        `SELECT EXISTS(\n485\t           SELECT 1\n486\t           FROM messages\n487\t           WHERE id = $1\n488\t             AND \"channelId\" = $2\n489\t             AND \"createdAt\" = $3::timestamptz\n490\t             AND \"isDeleted\" = false\n491\t        ) AS exists`,\n492\t        [record.messageId, record.channelId, record.messageCreatedAt],\n493\t      );\n494\t      if (!booleanColumn(messageRows, 'exists')) {\n495\t        return null;\n496\t      }\n497\t\n498\t      return {\n499\t        channelId: record.channelId,\n500\t        messageId: record.messageId,\n501\t        messageCreatedAt: record.messageCreatedAt,\n502\t      };\n503\t    });\n504\t  }\n505\t\n506\t  /**\n507\t   * Handles user deletion: anonymises messages, removes channel memberships,\n508\t   * and cleans up reactions/receipts in a single transaction.\n509\t   * Pins tenant schema before executing any queries.\n510\t   */\n511\t  @EventPattern('events.*.UserDeleted')\n512\t  async handleUserDeleted(@Payload() data: UserDeletedPayload): Promise<void> {\n513\t    const deletedUserId = data.deletedUserId;\n514\t    if (!TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.tenantId) || !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(deletedUserId)) {\n515\t      this.logger.error(\n516\t        'Rejected UserDeleted event missing canonical deletedUserId or valid tenantId',\n517\t      );\n518\t      return;\n519\t    }\n520\t\n521\t    this.logger.log(\n522\t      `Processing UserDeleted for deletedUserId ${deletedUserId} in tenant ${data.tenantId}`,\n523\t    );\n524\t\n525\t    try {\n526\t      // MSG-CRITICAL-058: attachment MinIO object keys collected inside the tx for\n527\t      // the erased (non-held) messages; purged AFTER commit so the DB row is already\n528\t      // gone before we drop the binary (a failed purge leaves an orphan, not a live\n529\t      // PII reference). Held channels are excluded — legal hold preserves their media.\n530\t      const objectKeysToPurge = await this.withTenantQueryRunner(\n531\t        data.tenantId,\n532\t        async (queryRunner) => {\n533\t          // SECURITY: Verify user has actual presence in claimed tenant before destructive cascade\n534\t          const userMessages: unknown = await queryRunner.query(\n535\t            `SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM messages WHERE \"senderId\" = $1 LIMIT 1) AS has_messages`,\n536\t            [deletedUserId],\n537\t          );\n538\t          const userMemberships: unknown = await queryRunner.query(\n539\t            `SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM channel_members WHERE \"userId\" = $1 LIMIT 1) AS has_memberships`,\n540\t            [deletedUserId],\n541\t          );\n542\t\n543\t          if (\n544\t            !booleanColumn(userMessages, 'has_messages') &&\n545\t            !booleanColumn(userMemberships, 'has_memberships')\n546\t          ) {\n547\t            this.logger.log(\n548\t              `UserDeleted: deletedUserId=${deletedUserId} has no messaging footprint in tenant ${data.tenantId}, skipping cascade`,\n549\t            );\n550\t            return [];\n551\t          }\n552\t\n553\t          // Collect ALL message IDs for this user BEFORE any anonymization.\n554\t          // WHY: after we set senderId=ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, we can no longer identify\n555\t          // which messages belonged to this specific user — ANONYMOUS_USER_ID is shared\n556\t          // across all deleted users. We need the IDs up front to clean AI-derived tables.\n557\t          const userMsgRowsResult: unknown = await queryRunner.query(\n558\t            `SELECT id, \"channelId\" FROM messages WHERE \"senderId\" = $1`,\n559\t            [deletedUserId],\n560\t          );\n561\t          const userMsgRows = Array.isArray(userMsgRowsResult)\n562\t            ? userMsgRowsResult.filter(isMessageChannelRow)\n563\t            : [];\n564\t          const userMessageIds = userMsgRows.map((r) => r.id);\n565\t\n566\t          // Determine which channels the user has messages in\n567\t          const channelRows = userMsgRows.reduce<Array<{ channelId: string }>>((acc, r) => {\n568\t            if (!acc.some((x) => x.channelId === r.channelId)) {\n569\t              acc.push({ channelId: r.channelId });\n570\t            }\n571\t            return acc;\n572\t          }, []);\n573\t\n574\t          // For each channel, check legal hold status and anonymize accordingly.\n575\t          // BEFORE: all messages wiped unconditionally — messages in litigation-held\n576\t          // channels had content destroyed, creating spoliation liability.\n577\t          // WHY: Legal hold requires content preservation. We still anonymize the senderId\n578\t          // (to protect the user's identity under GDPR) but preserve content in held channels.\n579\t          const heldChannelIds = new Set<string>();\n580\t          for (const { channelId } of channelRows) {\n581\t            const isHeld = await this.legalHoldService.isUnderLegalHold(data.tenantId, channelId);\n582\t            if (isHeld) {\n583\t              heldChannelIds.add(channelId);\n584\t              // Held channel: anonymize sender identity only, preserve content\n585\t              await queryRunner.query(\n586\t                `UPDATE messages SET \"senderId\" = $1 WHERE \"senderId\" = $2 AND \"channelId\" = $3`,\n587\t                [ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, deletedUserId, channelId],\n588\t              );\n589\t            }\n590\t          }\n591\t\n592\t          // MSG-CRITICAL-058: for messages in NON-HELD channels (whose content we are\n593\t          // about to wipe), delete the attachment rows and collect their MinIO object\n594\t          // keys so the binaries are purged after commit. Held-channel attachments are\n595\t          // preserved — legal hold outranks erasure. Mirrors GdprService.anonymizeMyData.\n596\t          const erasableMessageIds = userMsgRows\n597\t            .filter((r) => !heldChannelIds.has(r.channelId))\n598\t            .map((r) => r.id);\n599\t          const objectKeys: string[] = [];\n600\t          if (erasableMessageIds.length > 0) {\n601\t            const attachmentRows: Array<{ storageKey: string; thumbnailKey: string | null }> =\n602\t              await queryRunner.query(\n603\t                `SELECT \"storageKey\", \"thumbnailKey\" FROM message_attachments\n604\t               WHERE \"messageId\" = ANY($1::uuid[])`,\n605\t                [erasableMessageIds],\n606\t              );\n607\t            for (const row of attachmentRows) {\n608\t              if (row.storageKey) objectKeys.push(row.storageKey);\n609\t              if (row.thumbnailKey) objectKeys.push(row.thumbnailKey);\n610\t            }\n611\t            await queryRunner.query(\n612\t              `DELETE FROM message_attachments WHERE \"messageId\" = ANY($1::uuid[])`,\n613\t              [erasableMessageIds],\n614\t            );\n615\t          }\n616\t\n617\t          // Non-held channels: anonymize sender + wipe content + clear embedding.\n618\t          // BEFORE: embedding column was NOT cleared — vector index retained the user's\n619\t          // original message content even after anonymization, enabling re-identification\n620\t          // via semantic similarity search. GdprService.anonymizeMyData() correctly\n621\t          // sets embedding=NULL; this handler now aligns with that behavior.\n622\t          if (heldChannelIds.size < channelRows.length) {\n623\t            const heldIds = Array.from(heldChannelIds);\n624\t            const whereClause =\n625\t              heldIds.length > 0\n626\t                ? `\"senderId\" = $2 AND \"channelId\" != ALL($3::uuid[])`\n627\t                : `\"senderId\" = $2`;\n628\t            const params =\n629\t              heldIds.length > 0\n630\t                ? [ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, deletedUserId, heldIds]\n631\t                : [ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, deletedUserId];\n632\t\n633\t            await queryRunner.query(\n634\t              `UPDATE messages\n635\t             SET \"senderId\" = $1,\n636\t                 content = '[message deleted by user]',\n637\t                 embedding = NULL\n638\t             WHERE ${whereClause}`,\n639\t              params,\n640\t            );\n641\t          }\n642\t\n643\t          // Clean AI-derived PII using the message IDs collected before anonymization.\n644\t          // BEFORE: message_entity_references and message_analysis rows were never cleaned.\n645\t          // Also: there was a bug where IDs were collected AFTER anonymization by querying\n646\t          // senderId=ANONYMOUS_USER_ID — which would return ALL anonymized users' messages.\n647\t          // Using pre-collected userMessageIds is the correct approach.\n648\t          if (userMessageIds.length > 0) {\n649\t            await queryRunner.query(\n650\t              `DELETE FROM message_entity_references WHERE \"messageId\" = ANY($1::uuid[])`,\n651\t              [userMessageIds],\n652\t            );\n653\t            await queryRunner.query(\n654\t              `DELETE FROM message_analysis WHERE \"messageId\" = ANY($1::uuid[])`,\n655\t              [userMessageIds],\n656\t            );\n657\t          }\n658\t\n659\t          // Remove reactions\n660\t          await queryRunner.query(`DELETE FROM message_reactions WHERE \"userId\" = $1`, [\n661\t            deletedUserId,\n662\t          ]);\n663\t\n664\t          // Remove receipts\n665\t          await queryRunner.query(`DELETE FROM message_receipts WHERE \"userId\" = $1`, [\n666\t            deletedUserId,\n667\t          ]);\n668\t\n669\t          // Mark channel memberships as left\n670\t          await queryRunner.query(\n671\t            `UPDATE channel_members SET \"leftAt\" = NOW() WHERE \"userId\" = $1 AND \"leftAt\" IS NULL`,\n672\t            [deletedUserId],\n673\t          );\n674\t\n675\t          return objectKeys;\n676\t        },\n677\t      );\n678\t\n679\t      // MSG-CRITICAL-058: the attachment ROWS are committed-deleted; purge the MinIO\n680\t      // binaries (best-effort, post-commit). Tenant-prefix isolation is enforced\n681\t      // inside purgeObjects. A failed purge orphans the object for a reaper — it does\n682\t      // not leave a live, resolvable PII reference.\n683\t      if (objectKeysToPurge.length > 0) {\n684\t        const purge = await this.attachmentObjectPurge.purgeObjects(\n685\t          data.tenantId,\n686\t          objectKeysToPurge,\n687\t        );\n688\t        if (purge.failed > 0) {\n689\t          this.logger.error(\n690\t            `UserDeleted erasure: ${purge.failed}/${purge.requested} attachment object(s) failed ` +\n691\t              `to delete for tenant ${data.tenantId}; orphaned objects require reaper cleanup`,\n692\t          );\n693\t        }\n694\t      }\n695\t      this.logger.log(`UserDeleted cascade completed for deletedUserId ${deletedUserId}`);\n696\t    } catch (err: unknown) {\n697\t      this.logger.error(`UserDeleted cascade failed for ${deletedUserId}: ${errorMessage(err)}`);\n698\t    }\n699\t  }\n700\t\n701\t  /**\n702\t   * SEC-M17: When a new tenant is provisioned, create messaging partitions for its schema.\n703\t   *\n704\t   * Validates both tenantId (UUID) and schemaName (canonical tenant schema) from the\n705\t   * NATS payload before processing. Rejects payloads with invalid formats to prevent\n706\t   * SQL injection via crafted NATS messages from compromised containers.\n707\t   *\n708\t   * Subject shape (ORPHAN-HIGH-317 remediation): the publisher emits the\n709\t   * canonical 3-segment `events.{tenantId}.TenantProvisioned`"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e146bd02-c5f5-4f1e-952e-b26450ca601a","uuid":"4a7db81a-caf3-4f06-b2b1-19a8e195bcb3","timestamp":"2026-10-06T10:02:16.838Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-consensus-arbiter-6a58206c561b/apps/messaging-service/src/event-handlers/messaging-nats.handler.ts","content":" up reactions/receipts in a single transaction.\n   * Pins tenant schema before executing any queries.\n   */\n  @EventPattern('events.*.UserDeleted')\n  async handleUserDeleted(@Payload() data: UserDeletedPayload): Promise<void> {\n    const deletedUserId = data.deletedUserId;\n    if (!TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.tenantId) || !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(deletedUserId)) {\n      this.logger.error(\n        'Rejected UserDeleted event missing canonical deletedUserId or valid tenantId',\n      );\n      return;\n    }\n\n    this.logger.log(\n      `Processing UserDeleted for deletedUserId ${deletedUserId} in tenant ${data.tenantId}`,\n    );\n\n    try {\n      // MSG-CRITICAL-058: attachment MinIO object keys collected inside the tx for\n      // the erased (non-held) messages; purged AFTER commit so the DB row is already\n      // gone before we drop the binary (a failed purge leaves an orphan, not a live\n      // PII reference). Held channels are excluded — legal hold preserves their media.\n      const objectKeysToPurge = await this.withTenantQueryRunner(\n        data.tenantId,\n        async (queryRunner) => {\n          // SECURITY: Verify user has actual presence in claimed tenant before destructive cascade\n          const userMessages: unknown = await queryRunner.query(\n            `SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM messages WHERE \"senderId\" = $1 LIMIT 1) AS has_messages`,\n            [deletedUserId],\n          );\n          const userMemberships: unknown = await queryRunner.query(\n            `SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM channel_members WHERE \"userId\" = $1 LIMIT 1) AS has_memberships`,\n            [deletedUserId],\n          );\n\n          if (\n            !booleanColumn(userMessages, 'has_messages') &&\n            !booleanColumn(userMemberships, 'has_memberships')\n          ) {\n            this.logger.log(\n              `UserDeleted: deletedUserId=${deletedUserId} has no messaging footprint in tenant ${data.tenantId}, skipping cascade`,\n            );\n            return [];\n          }\n\n          // Collect ALL message IDs for this user BEFORE any anonymization.\n          // WHY: after we set senderId=ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, we can no longer identify\n          // which messages belonged to this specific user — ANONYMOUS_USER_ID is shared\n          // across all deleted users. We need the IDs up front to clean AI-derived tables.\n          const userMsgRowsResult: unknown = await queryRunner.query(\n            `SELECT id, \"channelId\" FROM messages WHERE \"senderId\" = $1`,\n            [deletedUserId],\n          );\n          const userMsgRows = Array.isArray(userMsgRowsResult)\n            ? userMsgRowsResult.filter(isMessageChannelRow)\n            : [];\n          const userMessageIds = userMsgRows.map((r) => r.id);\n\n          // Determine which channels the user has messages in\n          const channelRows = userMsgRows.reduce<Array<{ channelId: string }>>((acc, r) => {\n            if (!acc.some((x) => x.channelId === r.channelId)) {\n              acc.push({ channelId: r.channelId });\n            }\n            return acc;\n          }, []);\n\n          // For each channel, check legal hold status and anonymize accordingly.\n          // BEFORE: all messages wiped unconditionally — messages in litigation-held\n          // channels had content destroyed, creating spoliation liability.\n          // WHY: Legal hold requires content preservation. We still anonymize the senderId\n          // (to protect the user's identity under GDPR) but preserve content in held channels.\n          const heldChannelIds = new Set<string>();\n          for (const { channelId } of channelRows) {\n            const isHeld = await this.legalHoldService.isUnderLegalHold(data.tenantId, channelId);\n            if (isHeld) {\n              heldChannelIds.add(channelId);\n              // Held channel: anonymize sender identity only, preserve content\n              await queryRunner.query(\n                `UPDATE messages SET \"senderId\" = $1 WHERE \"senderId\" = $2 AND \"channelId\" = $3`,\n                [ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, deletedUserId, channelId],\n              );\n            }\n          }\n\n          // MSG-CRITICAL-058: for messages in NON-HELD channels (whose content we are\n          // about to wipe), delete the attachment rows and collect their MinIO object\n          // keys so the binaries are purged after commit. Held-channel attachments are\n          // preserved — legal hold outranks erasure. Mirrors GdprService.anonymizeMyData.\n          const erasableMessageIds = userMsgRows\n            .filter((r) => !heldChannelIds.has(r.channelId))\n            .map((r) => r.id);\n          const objectKeys: string[] = [];\n          if (erasableMessageIds.length > 0) {\n            const attachmentRows: Array<{ storageKey: string; thumbnailKey: string | null }> =\n              await queryRunner.query(\n                `SELECT \"storageKey\", \"thumbnailKey\" FROM message_attachments\n               WHERE \"messageId\" = ANY($1::uuid[])`,\n                [erasableMessageIds],\n              );\n            for (const row of attachmentRows) {\n              if (row.storageKey) objectKeys.push(row.storageKey);\n              if (row.thumbnailKey) objectKeys.push(row.thumbnailKey);\n            }\n            await queryRunner.query(\n              `DELETE FROM message_attachments WHERE \"messageId\" = ANY($1::uuid[])`,\n              [erasableMessageIds],\n            );\n          }\n\n          // Non-held channels: anonymize sender + wipe content + clear embedding.\n          // BEFORE: embedding column was NOT cleared — vector index retained the user's\n          // original message content even after anonymization, enabling re-identification\n          // via semantic similarity search. GdprService.anonymizeMyData() correctly\n          // sets embedding=NULL; this handler now aligns with that behavior.\n          if (heldChannelIds.size < channelRows.length) {\n            const heldIds = Array.from(heldChannelIds);\n            const whereClause =\n              heldIds.length > 0\n                ? `\"senderId\" = $2 AND \"channelId\" != ALL($3::uuid[])`\n                : `\"senderId\" = $2`;\n            const params =\n              heldIds.length > 0\n                ? [ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, deletedUserId, heldIds]\n                : [ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, deletedUserId];\n\n            await queryRunner.query(\n              `UPDATE messages\n             SET \"senderId\" = $1,\n                 content = '[message deleted by user]',\n                 embedding = NULL\n             WHERE ${whereClause}`,\n              params,\n            );\n          }\n\n          // Clean AI-derived PII using the message IDs collected before anonymization.\n          // BEFORE: message_entity_references and message_analysis rows were never cleaned.\n          // Also: there was a bug where IDs were collected AFTER anonymization by querying\n          // senderId=ANONYMOUS_USER_ID — which would return ALL anonymized users' messages.\n          // Using pre-collected userMessageIds is the correct approach.\n          if (userMessageIds.length > 0) {\n            await queryRunner.query(\n              `DELETE FROM message_entity_references WHERE \"messageId\" = ANY($1::uuid[])`,\n              [userMessageIds],\n            );\n            await queryRunner.query(\n              `DELETE FROM message_analysis WHERE \"messageId\" = ANY($1::uuid[])`,\n              [userMessageIds],\n            );\n          }\n\n          // Remove reactions\n          await queryRunner.query(`DELETE FROM message_reactions WHERE \"userId\" = $1`, [\n            deletedUserId,\n          ]);\n\n          // Remove receipts\n          await queryRunner.query(`DELETE FROM message_receipts WHERE \"userId\" = $1`, [\n            deletedUserId,\n          ]);\n\n          // Mark channel memberships as left\n          await queryRunner.query(\n            `UPDATE channel_members SET \"leftAt\" = NOW() WHERE \"userId\" = $1 AND \"leftAt\" IS NULL`,\n            [deletedUserId],\n          );\n\n          return objectKeys;\n        },\n      );\n\n      // MSG-CRITICAL-058: the attachment ROWS are committed-deleted; purge the MinIO\n      // binaries (best-effort, post-commit). Tenant-prefix isolation is enforced\n      // inside purgeObjects. A failed purge orphans the object for a reaper — it does\n      // not leave a live, resolvable PII reference.\n      if (objectKeysToPurge.length > 0) {\n        const purge = await this.attachmentObjectPurge.purgeObjects(\n          data.tenantId,\n          objectKeysToPurge,\n        );\n        if (purge.failed > 0) {\n          this.logger.error(\n            `UserDeleted erasure: ${purge.failed}/${purge.requested} attachment object(s) failed ` +\n              `to delete for tenant ${data.tenantId}; orphaned objects require reaper cleanup`,\n          );\n        }\n      }\n      this.logger.log(`UserDeleted cascade completed for deletedUserId ${deletedUserId}`);\n    } catch (err: unknown) {\n      this.logger.error(`UserDeleted cascade failed for ${deletedUserId}: ${errorMessage(err)}`);\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * SEC-M17: When a new tenant is provisioned, create messaging partitions for its schema.\n   *\n   * Validates both tenantId (UUID) and schemaName (canonical tenant schema) from the\n   * NATS payload before processing. Rejects payloads with invalid formats to prevent\n   * SQL injection via crafted NATS messages from compromised containers.\n   *\n   * Subject shape (ORPHAN-HIGH-317 remediation): the publisher emits the\n   * canonical 3-segment `events.{tenantId}.TenantProvisioned`","numLines":230,"startLine":480,"totalLines":764}}}

A full\n147\t * TypeORM `QueryRunner` satisfies this, but narrowing to it keeps the assertion\n148\t * unit-testable with a minimal mock and no unsafe double cast.\n149\t */\n150\texport interface TenantContextQueryExecutor {\n151\t  query(sql: string, parameters?: unknown[]): Promise<unknown>;\n152\t}\n153\t\n154\t/**\n155\t * Bind the RLS controls transaction-locally and verify they took.\n156\t *\n157\t * ORPHAN-CRITICAL-573 — extracted from `assertTenantTransactionContext`\n158\t * because auth-service needs exactly this and NOT the schema half of it: its\n159\t * tenant lifecycle writes land in the SOURCE schema (`auth.*`), and during\n160\t * provisioning the tenant schema does not exist yet. Calling the full\n161\t * assertion there would trade one failure for another.\n162\t *\n163\t * What it guarantees: `app.current_tenant` is this tenant, `app.bypass_rls`\n164\t * is off, both transaction-locally so a pooled connection cannot carry either\n165\t * into the next caller — and both READ BACK, so a GUC that silently failed to\n166\t * apply becomes a named `TenantContextError` instead of an RLS refusal whose\n167\t * message names a table and not a cause. That distinction is the whole reason\n168\t * tenant onboarding stayed broken for months.\n169\t */\n170\texport async function bindTenantRlsContext(\n171\t  queryRunner: TenantContextQueryExecutor,\n172\t  tenantId: string,\n173\t  sourceSchema: string,\n174\t): Promise<void> {\n175\t  if (!SOURCE_SCHEMA_RE.test(sourceSchema)) {\n176\t    throw new Error(`bindTenantRlsContext: invalid source schema \"${sourceSchema}\"`);\n177\t  }\n178\t  if (!isValidUUID(tenantId)) {\n179\t    throw new Error(`bindTenantRlsContext: invalid tenantId \"${tenantId}\"`);\n180\t  }\n181\t\n182\t  await queryRunner.query(`SELECT set_config($1, $2, true)`, [RLS_TENANT_GUC, tenantId]);\n183\t  // A pooled connection may previously have served an audited system path;\n184\t  // a stale `on` would expose every row the policy is supposed to hide.\n185\t  await queryRunner.query(`SELECT set_config($1, 'off', true)`, [RLS_BYPASS_GUC]);\n186\t\n187\t  const rows = await queryRunner.query(\n188\t    `SELECT current_setting($1, true) AS tenant,\n189\t            current_setting($2, true) AS bypass`,\n190\t    [RLS_TENANT_GUC, RLS_BYPASS_GUC],\n191\t  );\n192\t  const row = (Array.isArray(rows) ? rows[0] : rows) as\n193\t    | { tenant?: string | null; bypass?: string | null }\n194\t    | undefined\n195\t    | null;\n196\t  if (!row) {\n197\t    return;\n198\t  }\n199\t\n200\t  const resolvedTenant: string | null = row.tenant ?? null;\n201\t  const resolvedBypass: string | null = row.bypass ?? null;\n202\t  if (resolvedTenant !== tenantId || (resolvedBypass ?? 'off') !== 'off') {\n203\t    throw new TenantContextError({\n204\t      state: 'RLS_MISMATCH',\n205\t      expectedSchema: getTenantSchemaName(tenantId),\n206\t      // No schema claim is being made for.\n209\t      resolvedSchema: null,\n210\t      sourceSchema,\n211\t    });\n212\t  }\n213\t}\n214\t\n215\texport async function assertTenantTransactionContext(\n216\t  queryRunner: TenantContextQueryExecutor,\n217\t  sourceSchema: string,\n218\t  tenantId: string,\n219\t): Promise<void> {\n220\t  if (!SOURCE_SCHEMA_RE.test(sourceSchema)) {\n221\t    throw new left\n230\t  // unset by a missing checkout patch or inherited stale from a pooled session.\n231\t  await queryRunner.query(`SELECT set_config($1, $2, true)`, [RLS_TENANT_GUC, tenantId]);\n232\t  // A off would leave a stale `on`\n235\t  // capable of exposing every row in the otherwise-correct tenant schema.\n236\t  await queryRunner.query(`SELECT set_config($1, 'off', true)`, queryRunner.query("}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e146bd02-c5f5-4f1e-952e-b26450ca601a","uuid":"fe5b041f-0ea5-4a08-b1eb-3d3e9e51e960","timestamp":"2026-10-06T10:02:18.183Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-consensus-arbiter-6a58206c561b/libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-transaction.ts","content":"  validateTenantSchemaName(tenantSchema);\n\n  await queryRunner.query(`SELECT pg_catalog.set_config('search_path', $1, true)`, [\n    `\"${tenantSchema}\", \"${sourceSchema}\", public`,\n  ]);\n}\n\n/**\n * Set the `app.current_tenant` RLS GUC transaction-locally and ASSERT that the\n * live connection actually resolves to the expected tenant schema + tenant id\n * BEFORE any domain query runs.\n *\n * This is the load-bearing fail-closed guarantee (Farm Data SSOT plan §5-1).\n * `RlsConnectionBootstrap` sets the GUC on pool checkout, but nothing today\n * verifies it actually took effect on the connection a handler ends up using —\n * so an unset GUC (RLS denies all rows) or a missing tenant schema (search_path\n * silently falls back to the source schema) both produce an empty result that\n * is indistinguishable from a legitimately-empty table. By owning the GUC\n * transaction-locally and reading back `current_schema()` + the GUC, the\n * boundary turns those two silent failure modes into a hard `TenantContextError`.\n *\n * `current_schema()` returns the first EXISTING schema in `search_path`, so a\n * missing/un-provisioned tenant schema (which falls through to the source\n * schema) is caught as a `SCHEMA_MISMATCH`.\n */\n/**\n * The single capability the boundary assertion needs from a connection. A full\n * TypeORM `QueryRunner` satisfies this, but narrowing to it keeps the assertion\n * unit-testable with a minimal mock and no unsafe double cast.\n */\nexport interface TenantContextQueryExecutor {\n  query(sql: string, parameters?: unknown[]): Promise<unknown>;\n}\n\n/**\n * Bind the RLS controls transaction-locally and verify they took.\n *\n * ORPHAN-CRITICAL-573 — extracted from `assertTenantTransactionContext`\n * because auth-service needs exactly this and NOT the schema half of it: its\n * tenant lifecycle writes land in the SOURCE schema (`auth.*`), and during\n * provisioning the tenant schema does not exist yet. Calling the full\n * assertion there would trade one failure for another.\n *\n * What it guarantees: `app.current_tenant` is this tenant, `app.bypass_rls`\n * is off, both transaction-locally so a pooled connection cannot carry either\n * into the next caller — and both READ BACK, so a GUC that silently failed to\n * apply becomes a named `TenantContextError` instead of an RLS refusal whose\n * message names a table and not a cause. That distinction is the whole reason\n * tenant onboarding stayed broken for months.\n */\nexport async function bindTenantRlsContext(\n  queryRunner: TenantContextQueryExecutor,\n  tenantId: string,\n  sourceSchema: string,\n): Promise<void> {\n  if (!SOURCE_SCHEMA_RE.test(sourceSchema)) {\n    throw new Error(`bindTenantRlsContext: invalid source schema \"${sourceSchema}\"`);\n  }\n  if (!isValidUUID(tenantId)) {\n    throw new Error(`bindTenantRlsContext: invalid tenantId \"${tenantId}\"`);\n  }\n\n  await queryRunner.query(`SELECT set_config($1, $2, true)`, [RLS_TENANT_GUC, tenantId]);\n  // A pooled connection may previously have served an audited system path;\n  // a stale `on` would expose every row the policy is supposed to hide.\n  await queryRunner.query(`SELECT set_config($1, 'off', true)`, [RLS_BYPASS_GUC]);\n\n  const rows = await queryRunner.query(\n    `SELECT current_setting($1, true) AS tenant,\n            current_setting($2, true) AS bypass`,\n    [RLS_TENANT_GUC, RLS_BYPASS_GUC],\n  );\n  const row = (Array.isArray(rows) ? rows[0] : rows) as\n    | { tenant?: string | null; bypass?: string | null }\n    | undefined\n    | null;\n  if (!row) {\n    return;\n  }\n\n  const resolvedTenant: string | null = row.tenant ?? null;\n  const resolvedBypass: string | null = row.bypass ?? null;\n  if (resolvedTenant !== tenantId || (resolvedBypass ?? 'off') !== 'off') {\n    throw new TenantContextError({\n      state: 'RLS_MISMATCH',\n      expectedSchema: getTenantSchemaName(tenantId),\n      // No schema claim is being made here: this binding is used by writers\n      // whose tables live in the source schema, so `resolvedSchema` stays\n      // null rather than asserting something the caller never asked for.\n      resolvedSchema: null,\n      sourceSchema,\n    });\n  }\n}\n\nexport async function assertTenantTransactionContext(\n  queryRunner: TenantContextQueryExecutor,\n  sourceSchema: string,\n  tenantId: string,\n): Promise<void> {\n  if (!SOURCE_SCHEMA_RE.test(sourceSchema)) {\n    throw new Error(`assertTenantTransactionContext: invalid source schema \"${sourceSchema}\"`);\n  }\n  if (!isValidUUID(tenantId)) {\n    throw new Error(`assertTenantTransactionContext: invalid tenantId \"${tenantId}\"`);\n  }\n\n  const expectedSchema = getTenantSchemaName(tenantId);\n\n  // The boundary OWNS the RLS GUC transaction-locally so it cannot be left\n  // unset by a missing checkout patch or inherited stale from a pooled session.\n  await queryRunner.query(`SELECT set_config($1, $2, true)`, [RLS_TENANT_GUC, tenantId]);\n  // A pooled connection may previously have served an audited system path.\n  // Tenant transactions own both RLS controls transaction-locally: setting\n  // the tenant UUID without forcing bypass off would leave a stale `on`\n  // capable of exposing every row in the otherwise-correct tenant schema.\n  await queryRunner.query(`SELECT set_config($1, 'off', true)`, [RLS_BYPASS_GUC]);\n\n  // Read back the schema + GUC the connection ACTUALLY resolves to.\n  const rows = await queryRunner.query(","numLines":120,"startLine":120,"totalLines":483}}}

*/\n@Controller()\nexport class MessagingNatsHandler {\n  private readonly logger = new Logger(MessagingNatsHandler.name);\n\n  constructor(\n    @InjectRepository(ChannelMember)\n    private readonly interpolation to\n   * prevent SQL injection via crafted NATS messages from compromised containers.\n   *\n   * @throws Error if tenantId does not match UUID v4 format\n   */\n  private async setTenantSchema(queryRunner: QueryRunner, tenantId: string): Promise<void> {\n    if (!TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(tenantId)) {\n      throw new Error(`SEC-M17: Invalid tenant ID format rejected: ${tenantId.substring(0, 50)}`);\n    }\n    const schemaName = getTenantSchemaName(tenantId);\n    await queryRunner.query(`SELECT pg_catalog.set_config('search_path', $1, true)`, [\n      `\"${schemaName}\", \"messaging\", public`,\n    ]);\n  }\n\n  private async withTenantQueryRunner<T>(\n    tenantId: string,\n    work: (queryRunner: QueryRunner) => Promise<T>,\n  ): Promise<T> {\n    const queryRunner = this.dataSource.createQueryRunner();\n    await queryRunner.connect();\n    await queryRunner.startTransaction();\n\n    try {\n      await this.setTenantSchema(queryRunner, tenantId);\n      // MSGFIX-FAZ1 (live-diagnosis root cause): search_path ALONE is not\n      // enough — the tenant/messaging schema tables sit under FORCE RLS\n      // with tenant_isolation_policy: a row is visible only when\n      // app.bypass_rls='on' OR \"tenantId\" = app.current_tenant. This path\n      // (unlike the HTTP TenantGuard) never set the GUC, so every NATS\n      // request-reply handler (verifyMembership, getChannelMembers,\n      // getMessageForBroadcast, resolveNotificationRef) silently read 0\n      // rows — WS joins were denied with \"not a member\" and hydration\n      // returned null (evidence: DEPLOY-FAZ1.md §3). bindTenantRlsContext\n      // sets the GUC transaction-locally AND reads it back (also forcing a\n      // stale bypass 'off') — the canonical backend-common pattern.\n      await bindTenantRlsContext(queryRunner, tenantId, 'messaging');\n      const result = await work(queryRunner);\n      await queryRunner.commitTransaction();\n      return result;\n    } catch (error) {\n      if (queryRunner.isTransactionActive) {\n        try {\n          await queryRunner.rollbackTransaction();\n        } catch (rollbackError: unknown) {\n          this.logger.error(\n            `Tenant NATS transaction rollback failed: ${errorMessage(rollbackError)}`,\n          );\n        }\n      }\n      throw error;\n    } finally {\n      await queryRunner.release();\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Verify that a user is an active member of a channel.\n   * Used by the WebSocket gateway for join-room authorisation.\n   */\n  @MessagePattern('request.messaging.verifyMembership')\n  async verifyMembership(@Payload() data: VerifyMembershipPayload): Promise<boolean> {\n    return this.withTenantQueryRunner(data.tenantId, async (queryRunner) => {\n      // MSGFIX-FAZ1: explicit tenantId predicate (the GraphQL-side pattern —\n      // message.resolver.ts validateChannelMembership). RLS\n      // (bindTenantRlsContext) already fences the rows; the predicate is\n      // the second defense layer.\n      const member = await queryRunner.manager.findOne(ChannelMember, {\n        where: {\n          tenantId: data.tenantId,\n          channelId: data.channelId,\n          userId: data.userId,\n          leftAt: IsNull(),\n        },\n      });\n      return !!member;\n    });\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Returns the list of active members for a given channel.\n   * Used by notification-service to determine push-notification recipients.\n   */\n  @MessagePattern('request.messaging.getChannelMembers')\n  async getChannelMembers(@Payload() data: GetChannelMembersPayload): Promise<ChannelMemberDto[]> {\n    return GetMessageBatchPayload): Promise<MessageBatchDto[]> {\n    if (!data.messageIds || data.messageIds.length === 0) return [];\n\n    return this.withTenantQueryRunner(data.tenantId, async (queryRunner) => {\n      const messages = await queryRunner.manager.find(Message, {\n        where: { id: In(data.messageIds), isDeleted: false },\n      });\n\n      return messages.map((m) => ({\n        id: m.id,\n        channelId: m.channelId,\n        senderId: m.senderId,\n        content: m.content,\n        contentType: m.contentType,\n        createdAt: m.createdAt,\n      }));\n    });\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Hydrate one message into the full {@link WsMessage} the gateway WS bridge\n   * broadcasts (MSG-CRITICAL-050). The NATS domain events are thin (IDs only)\n   * per ADR-006; the bridge cannot emit a body the client can render without\n   * this hydration step.\n   *\n   * SECURITY: the response carries `sender: { id }` ONLY — display PII\n   * (name/avatar) is deliberately NOT exposed over NATS (auth-user-queries\n   * profile-oracle constraint). The client enriches the sender from its\n   * channel-members cache (loaded over the authorized GraphQL federation path).\n   * Attachments carry tenant-scoped presigned URLs (the same isolation guard as\n   * the GraphQL field resolver). Returns `{ message: null }` when the message is\n   * absent in the tenant — the bridge then drops the broadcast.\n   */\n  @MessagePattern(GET_MESSAGE_FOR_BROADCAST_SUBJECT)\n  async getMessageForBroadcast(\n    @Payload() data: GetMessageForBroadcastRequest,\n  ): Promise<GetMessageForBroadcastResponse> {\n    if (\n      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.tenantId) ||\n      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.channelId) ||\n      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.messageId)\n    ) {\n      this.logger.warn('Rejected getMessageForBroadcast with invalid id format');\n      return { message: null };\n    }\n\n    return this.withTenantQueryRunner(data.tenantId, async (queryRunner) => {\n      const message = await queryRunner.manager.findOne(Message, {\n        where: { id: data.messageId, channelId: data.channelId, isDeleted: false },\n      });\n      if (!message) {\n        return { message: null };\n      }\n\n      const [attachments, receipts] = await Promise.all([\n        queryRunner.manager.find(MessageAttachment, {\n          where: {\n            tenantId: data.tenantId,\n            messageId: message.id,\n            messageCreatedAt: message.createdAt,\n            isDeleted: false,\n          },\n          order: { createdAt: 'ASC' },\n        }),\n        queryRunner.manager.find(MessageReceipt, {\n          where: {\n            tenantId: data.tenantId,\n            messageId: message.id,\n            messageCreatedAt: message.createdAt,\n          },\n          order: { receiptCreatedAt: 'ASC' },\n        }),\n      ]);\n\n      const wsAttachments: WsMessageAttachment[] = await Promise.all(\n        attachments.map(async (a) => ({\n          id: a.id,\n          originalFilename: a.originalFilename,\n          mimeType: a.mimeType,\n          fileSize: Number(a.fileSize),\n          width: a.width,\n          height: a.height,\n          durationSeconds: a.durationSeconds,\n          downloadUrl: await this.safePresign(data.tenantId, a.storageKey),\n          thumbnailUrl: await this.safePresign(data.tenantId, a.thumbnailKey),\n        })),\n      );\n\n      const wsReceipts: WsMessageReceipt[] = exactly\n        // (S1-CODEGEN). {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KTkUP8Rw9cehZck7ykfsdN","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t#!/usr/bin/env ts-node\n2\timport { relative } from 'node:path';\n3\t\n4\timport ts from 'typescript';\n5\t\n6\timport {\n7\t  collectFiles,\n8\t  filterFilesBySnapshot,\n9\t  normalizeWorkspacePath,\n10\t  readWorkspaceFile,\n11\t  requireScanRoots,\n12\t  resolveInsideWorkspace as resolveAdapterPath,\n13\t  workspacePathExists,\n14\t} from './adapter-fs';\n15\t\n16\ttype FindingRule =\n17\t  | 'tenant_repository_unscoped_read'\n18\t  | 'tenant_raw_query_missing_tenant_predicate'\n19\t  | 'tenant_guard_missing';\n20\t\n21\tinterface AdapterInput {\n22\t  readonly roots?: readonly string[];\n23\t  readonly allowlist?: readonly string[];\n24\t  readonly includeRepositoryReadFindings?: boolean;\n25\t  readonly repo_snapshot?: { readonly allowed_paths?: readonly string[]; readonly snapshot_hash?: string; readonly repo_state_id?: string };\n26\t}\n27\t\n28\tinterface EvidenceRef {\n29\t  readonly path: string;\n30\t  readonly line?: number;\n31\t}\n32\t\n33\tinterface AdapterObservation {\n34\t  readonly id: string;\n35\t  readonly type: string;\n36\t  readonly path?: string;\n37\t  readonly line?: number;\n38\t  readonly name?: string;\n39\t  readonly details?: Record<string, unknown>;\n40\t}\n41\t\n42\tinterface AdapterFinding {\n43\t  readonly id: string;\n44\t  readonly rule: FindingRule;\n45\t  readonly severity: 'medium' | 'high';\n46\t  readonly path: string;\n47\t  readonly line?: number;\n48\t  readonly message: string;\n49\t  readonly evidence: readonly EvidenceRef[];\n50\t  readonly confidence?: number;\n51\t  readonly actionability?: 'actionable' | 'review_required';\n52\t  readonly review_reason?: string;\n53\t  readonly details?: Record<string, unknown>;\n54\t}\n55\t\n56\tinterface MemoryCandidate {\n57\t  readonly belief_id: string;\n58\t  readonly claim: string;\n59\t  readonly confidence: number;\n60\t  readonly evidence_refs: readonly string[];\n61\t  readonly source_tool_id: string;\n62\t}\n63\t\n64\tinterface AriaOutput {\n65\t  readonly observations: readonly AdapterObservation[];\n66\t  readonly findings: readonly AdapterFinding[];\n67\t  readonly read_paths: readonly string[];\n68\t  readonly evidence_sources: readonly string[];\n69\t  readonly belief_candidates: readonly MemoryCandidate[];\n70\t  readonly cost_units: number;\n71\t  readonly metadata: Record<string, unknown>;\n72\t}\n73\t\n74\tinterface SourceUnit {\n75\t  readonly path: string;\n76\t  readonly relativePath: string;\n77\t  readonly text: string;\n78\t  readonly sourceFile: ts.SourceFile;\n79\t}\n80\t\n81\tinterface AnalysisContext {\n82\t  readonly checker: ts.TypeChecker;\n83\t  readonly tenantEntities: ReadonlyMap<string, TenantEntityRecord>;\n84\t  readonly tenantTables: ReadonlySet<string>;\n85\t  readonly includeRepositoryReadFindings: boolean;\n86\t}\n87\t\n88\tinterface TenantEntityRecord {\n89\t  readonly className: string;\n90\t  readonly tableName: string;\n91\t  readonly path: string;\n92\t  readonly line: number;\n93\t}\n94\t\n95\tinterface AnalysisResult {\n96\t  readonly observations: AdapterObservation[];\n97\t  readonly findings: AdapterFinding[];\n98\t  readonly readPaths: string[];\n99\t}\n100\t\n101\tconst REPOSITORY_METHODS = new Set(['find', 'findOne', 'findOneBy', 'findBy', 'count', 'update', 'delete', 'softDelete']);\n102\tconst RAW_QUERY_METHODS = new Set(['query']);\n103\tconst TENANT_GUARD_NAMES = new Set(['TenantGuard', 'GqlTenantGuard']);\n104\t\n105\texport function analyzeTenantScoping(input: AdapterInput, workspaceRoot = process.cwd()): AriaOutput {\n106\t  const roots = requireScanRoots('tenant-scoping-adapter', input.roots);\n107\t  const allowlist = new Set((input.allowlist ?? Narrow at the boundary rather\n552\t    // than widening `input` (kernel-dead-wire-adapter is the converged shape).\n553\t    process.stdin.on('data', (chunk: string | Buffer) => {\n554\t      input += typeof chunk === 'string' ? chunk : chunk.toString('utf8');\n555\t    });\n556\t    process.stdin.on('end', () => resolvePromise(input));\n557\t    process.stdin.on('error', reject);\n558\t  });\n559\t}\n560\t\n561\tasync function main(): Promise<void> {\n562\t  const rawInput = await readStdin();\n563\t  const input = rawInput.trim().length > 0 ? (JSON.parse(rawInput) as AdapterInput) : {};\n564\t  process.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify(analyzeTenantScoping(input))}\\n`);\n565\t}\n566\t\n567\tif (require.main === module) {\n568\t  main().catch((error: unknown) => {\n569\t    const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n570\t    process.stderr.write(`${message}\\n`);\n571\t    process.exit(1);\n572\t  });\n573\t}\n574\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e146bd02-c5f5-4f1e-952e-b26450ca601a","uuid":"473658bd-d36a-4252-8fc6-ff33a4e003e1","timestamp":"2026-10-06T10:03:28.302Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-consensus-arbiter-6a58206c561b/tools/aria-adapters/tenant-scoping-adapter.ts","content":"  };\n}\n\nfunction collectTypeScriptFiles(root: string): readonly string[] {\n  return collectFiles(root, {\n    extensions: ['.ts'],\n    includeExcludedDir: (name) => name === '__tests__',\n    includeFile: (name) => !isTestFile(name),\n  });\n}\n\nfunction isTestFile(name: string): boolean {\n  return name.endsWith('.spec.ts') || name.endsWith('.test.ts') || name.endsWith('.d.ts');\n}\n\nfunction resolveInsideWorkspace(workspaceRoot: string, requestedPath: string): string {\n  return resolveAdapterPath(workspaceRoot, requestedPath);\n}\n\nfunction lineOf(sourceFile: ts.SourceFile, node: ts.Node): number {\n  return sourceFile.getLineAndCharacterOfPosition(node.getStart(sourceFile)).line + 1;\n}\n\nfunction visit(node: ts.Node, visitor: (node: ts.Node) => void): void {\n  visitor(node);\n  ts.forEachChild(node, (child) => visit(child, visitor));\n}\n\nfunction compareById(a: { readonly id: string }, b: { readonly id: string }): number {\n  return a.id.localeCompare(b.id);\n}\n\nfunction normalizePath(path: string): string {\n  return normalizeWorkspacePath(path);\n}\n\nfunction readStdin(): Promise<string> {\n  return new Promise((resolvePromise, reject) => {\n    let input = '';\n    process.stdin.setEncoding('utf8');\n    // setEncoding('utf8') makes every chunk a string at runtime, but the\n    // stream's declared chunk type stays `string | Buffer` — concatenating the\n    // union is what the type checker rejects. Narrow at the boundary rather\n    // than widening `input` (kernel-dead-wire-adapter is the converged shape).\n    process.stdin.on('data', (chunk: string | Buffer) => {\n      input += typeof chunk === 'string' ? chunk : chunk.toString('utf8');\n    });\n    process.stdin.on('end', () => resolvePromise(input));\n    process.stdin.on('error', reject);\n  });\n}\n\nasync function main(): Promise<void> {\n  const rawInput = await readStdin();\n  const input = rawInput.trim().length > 0 ? (JSON.parse(rawInput) as AdapterInput) : {};\n  process.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify(analyzeTenantScoping(input))}\\n`);\n}\n\nif (require.main === module) {\n  main().catch((error: unknown) => {\n    const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n    process.stderr.write(`${message}\\n`);\n    process.exit(1);\n  });\n}\n","numLines":66,"startLine":509,"totalLines":574}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01E6A3uxUysombRLqP4QGvoA","type":"tool_result","content":"470\t  } else {\n471\t    const schemaRows = await queryRows<{ schema: string }>(\n472\t      qr,\n473\t      `SELECT current_schema() AS schema`,\n474\t    );\n475\t    schema = schemaRows[0]?.schema ?? 'public';\n476\t  }\n477\t  assertSafeIdentifier(schema, options.schemaOverride !== undefined ? 'schemaOverride' : 'current_schema');\n478\t  const skipTimescaleColumnstoreTables =\n479\t    options.skipTimescaleColumnstoreTables ?? TENANT_SCHEMA_REGEX.test(schema);\n480\t\n481\t  logger.log(\n482\t    `Applying tenant RLS in schema \"${schema}\" ` +\n483\t      `(columns: ${tenantIdColumns.join(',')}, include: ${includeTables.join(',') || '∅'}, exclude: ${excludeTables.join(',') || '∅'})`,\n484\t  );\n485\t\n486\t  const tables = await discoverTenantScopedTables(\n487\t    qr,\n488\t    schema,\n489\t    tenantIdColumns,\n490\t    excludeTables,\n491\t    includeTables,\n492\t    logger,\n493\t  );\n494\t\n495\t  if (tables.length === 0) {\n496\t    logger.warn(\n497\t      `No tenant-scoped tables found in schema \"${schema}\" — nothing to do. ` +\n498\t        `Check tenantIdColumns option if this is unexpected.`,\n499\t    );\n500\t    return;\n501\t  }\n502\t\n503\t  logger.log(`Discovered ${tables.length} tenant-scoped tables in \"${schema}\"`);\n504\t\n505\t  const columnstoreTables = skipTimescaleColumnstoreTables\n506\t    ? await discoverTimescaleColumnstoreTables(qr, schema, logger)\n507\t    : new Set<string>();\n508\t\n509\t  let applied = 0;\n510\t  let skipped = 0;\n511\t  for (const { tableName, tenantColumn } of tables) {\n512\t    assertSafeIdentifier(tableName, 'tableName');\n513\t\n514\t    if (columnstoreTables.has(tableName)) {\n515\t      skipped++;\n516\t      logger.warn(\n517\t        `[apply-tenant-rls] Skipping TimescaleDB columnstore hypertable ` +\n518\t          `\"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" because PostgreSQL RLS DDL is not ` +\n519\t          `supported while columnstore/compression is enabled. Tenant schema ` +\n520\t          `isolation remains the enforcement boundary for this table.`,\n521\t      );\n522\t      continue;\n523\t    }\n524\t    // Step 1: ENABLE then FORCE RLS. ENABLE turns it on for non-owners, FORCE\n525\t    // extends it to the table owner. We need both because the application\n526\t    // connects as the schema owner (`aquaculture`).\n527\t    await qr.query(\n528\t      `${SQL_ALTER_TABLE} \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" ENABLE ${SQL_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY}`,\n529\t    );\n530\t    await qr.query(\n531\t      `${SQL_ALTER_TABLE} \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" FORCE ${SQL_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY}`,\n532\t    );\n533\t\n534\t    // Step 2: drop any pre-existing policy with the canonical name. This is\n535\t    // what makes the helper a forward-migration tool — predicate changes\n536\t    // (like the NULLIF fix) are applied by simply re-running the helper.\n537\t    await qr.query(\n538\t      `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS \"${TENANT_ISOLATION_POLICY_NAME}\" ` +\n539\t        `ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\"`,\n540\t    );\n541\t\n542\t    // Step 3: create the policy with the canonical bypass-aware predicate.\n543\t    // FOR ALL covers SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE — for INSERT and UPDATE the\n544\t    // USING clause acts as the WITH CHECK clause too, so writers cannot\n545\t    // insert rows for other tenants either.\n546\t    const usingClause = buildTenantPolicyUsingClause(tenantColumn);\n547\t    await qr.query(\n548\t      `${SQL_CREATE_POLICY} \"${TENANT_ISOLATION_POLICY_NAME}\" ` +\n549\t        `ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" ` +\n550\t        `FOR ALL ` +\n551\t        `USING ${usingClause} ` +\n552\t        `WITH CHECK ${usingClause}`,\n553\t    );\n554\t\n555\t    logger.log(\n556\t      `RLS armed on \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" (col: ${tenantColumn})`,\n557\t    );\n558\t    applied++;\n559\t  }\n560\t\n561\t  logger.log(\n562\t    `Tenant RLS applied to ${applied} tables in schema \"${schema}\" (skipped: ${skipped})`,\n563\t  );\n564\t}\n565\t\n566\t/**\n567\t * Reverse `applyTenantRlsToSchema` — drops the canonical policy and disables\n568\t * RLS on every tenant-scoped table in the current schema. Used by migration\n569\t * `down()` methods so that rollbacks restore the pre-RLS state.\n570\t *\n571\t * Idempotent: missing policies and already-disabled tables are no-ops.\n572\t */\n573\texport async function removeTenantRlsFromSchema(\n574\t  qr: QueryRunner,\n575\t  options: Pick<ApplyTenantRlsOptions, 'tenantIdColumns' | 'excludeTables' | 'includeTables' | 'logger'> = {},\n576\t): Promise<void> {\n577\t  assertDbMigrateDdlAuthority('removeTenantRlsFromSchema');\n578\t\n579\t  const logger = options.logger ?? new Logger('removeTenantRlsFromSchema');"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e146bd02-c5f5-4f1e-952e-b26450ca601a","uuid":"0b116a06-8a4c-4ab3-879e-9231208babaa","timestamp":"2026-10-06T10:04:08.380Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-consensus-arbiter-6a58206c561b/libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts","content":"  } else {\n    const schemaRows = await queryRows<{ schema: string }>(\n      qr,\n      `SELECT current_schema() AS schema`,\n    );\n    schema = schemaRows[0]?.schema ?? 'public';\n  }\n  assertSafeIdentifier(schema, options.schemaOverride !== undefined ? 'schemaOverride' : 'current_schema');\n  const skipTimescaleColumnstoreTables =\n    options.skipTimescaleColumnstoreTables ?? TENANT_SCHEMA_REGEX.test(schema);\n\n  logger.log(\n    `Applying tenant RLS in schema \"${schema}\" ` +\n      `(columns: ${tenantIdColumns.join(',')}, include: ${includeTables.join(',') || '∅'}, exclude: ${excludeTables.join(',') || '∅'})`,\n  );\n\n  const tables = await discoverTenantScopedTables(\n    qr,\n    schema,\n    tenantIdColumns,\n    excludeTables,\n    includeTables,\n    logger,\n  );\n\n  if (tables.length === 0) {\n    logger.warn(\n      `No tenant-scoped tables found in schema \"${schema}\" — nothing to do. ` +\n        `Check tenantIdColumns option if this is unexpected.`,\n    );\n    return;\n  }\n\n  logger.log(`Discovered ${tables.length} tenant-scoped tables in \"${schema}\"`);\n\n  const columnstoreTables = skipTimescaleColumnstoreTables\n    ? await discoverTimescaleColumnstoreTables(qr, schema, logger)\n    : new Set<string>();\n\n  let applied = 0;\n  let skipped = 0;\n  for (const { tableName, tenantColumn } of tables) {\n    assertSafeIdentifier(tableName, 'tableName');\n\n    if (columnstoreTables.has(tableName)) {\n      skipped++;\n      logger.warn(\n        `[apply-tenant-rls] Skipping TimescaleDB columnstore hypertable ` +\n          `\"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" because PostgreSQL RLS DDL is not ` +\n          `supported while columnstore/compression is enabled. Tenant schema ` +\n          `isolation remains the enforcement boundary for this table.`,\n      );\n      continue;\n    }\n    // Step 1: ENABLE then FORCE RLS. ENABLE turns it on for non-owners, FORCE\n    // extends it to the table owner. We need both because the application\n    // connects as the schema owner (`aquaculture`).\n    await qr.query(\n      `${SQL_ALTER_TABLE} \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" ENABLE ${SQL_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY}`,\n    );\n    await qr.query(\n      `${SQL_ALTER_TABLE} \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" FORCE ${SQL_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY}`,\n    );\n\n    // Step 2: drop any pre-existing policy with the canonical name. This is\n    // what makes the helper a forward-migration tool — predicate changes\n    // (like the NULLIF fix) are applied by simply re-running the helper.\n    await qr.query(\n      `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS \"${TENANT_ISOLATION_POLICY_NAME}\" ` +\n        `ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\"`,\n    );\n\n    // Step 3: create the policy with the canonical bypass-aware predicate.\n    // FOR ALL covers SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE — for INSERT and UPDATE the\n    // USING clause acts as the WITH CHECK clause too, so writers cannot\n    // insert rows for other tenants either.\n    const usingClause = buildTenantPolicyUsingClause(tenantColumn);\n    await qr.query(\n      `${SQL_CREATE_POLICY} \"${TENANT_ISOLATION_POLICY_NAME}\" ` +\n        `ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" ` +\n        `FOR ALL ` +\n        `USING ${usingClause} ` +\n        `WITH CHECK ${usingClause}`,\n    );\n\n    logger.log(\n      `RLS armed on \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" (col: ${tenantColumn})`,\n    );\n    applied++;\n  }\n\n  logger.log(\n    `Tenant RLS applied to ${applied} tables in schema \"${schema}\" (skipped: ${skipped})`,\n  );\n}\n\n/**\n * Reverse `applyTenantRlsToSchema` — drops the canonical policy and disables\n * RLS on every tenant-scoped table in the current schema. Used by migration\n * `down()` methods so that rollbacks restore the pre-RLS state.\n *\n * Idempotent: missing policies and already-disabled tables are no-ops.\n */\nexport async function removeTenantRlsFromSchema(\n  qr: QueryRunner,\n  options: Pick<ApplyTenantRlsOptions, 'tenantIdColumns' | 'excludeTables' | 'includeTables' | 'logger'> = {},\n): Promise<void> {\n  assertDbMigrateDdlAuthority('removeTenantRlsFromSchema');\n\n  const logger = options.logger ?? new Logger('removeTenantRlsFromSchema');","numLines":110,"startLine":470,"totalLines":623}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01BDmaR6NLFPXHCgkasW5afx","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport { Logger } from '@nestjs/common';\n2\timport { QueryRunner } from 'typeorm';\n3\t\n4\t/**\n5\t * applyTenantRlsToSchema\n6\t * ============================================================================\n7\t *\n8\t * Bulk Row-Level Security (RLS) installer for tenant-scoped tables, designed\n9\t * to be invoked from a TypeORM `MigrationInterface.up()`.\n10\t *\n11\t * # Why a helper instead of per-service hand-written SQL?\n12\t *\n13\t * 1. **Single source of truth** for the RLS predicate. The predicate is\n14\t *    SECURITY-CRITICAL — a typo silently allows cross-tenant reads.\n15\t * 2. **Bug fix**: the previous farm-service migration used\n16\t *    `COALESCE(current_setting('app.current_tenant', true), '')::uuid` which\n17\t *    throws `invalid input syntax for type uuid: \"\"` whenever the GUC is\n18\t *    unset. We use `NULLIF(...)::uuid` so an unset GUC becomes NULL and the\n19\t *    comparison cleanly evaluates to UNKNOWN (no cast error, no rows leaked).\n20\t * 3. **Column-name flexibility**: the platform mixes naming conventions —\n21\t *    `farm-service.BaseEntity` uses `tenant_id` (snake_case), while\n22\t *    `auth-service.User` uses `\"tenantId\"` (camelCase quoted). Per-table\n23\t *    discovery handles both.\n24\t * 4. **Bypass clause** baked in: admin-api-service, background workers, and\n25\t *    cross-tenant analytics queries set `app.bypass_rls = 'on'` for the\n26\t *    duration of a transaction. The policy honours this without needing a\n27\t *    separate DB role (which would be operationally heavier).\n28\t * 5. **Idempotent**: safe to re-run after partial failures or in dev/staging\n29\t *    where the schema may already have policies installed (DROP IF EXISTS +\n30\t *    CREATE).\n31\t *\n32\t * # The RLS predicate (security-critical — read carefully)\n33\t *\n34\t * ```sql\n35\t * USING (\n36\t *   -- (1) Explicit bypass for SUPER_ADMIN/background-job contexts.\n37\t *   --     The caller must SET LOCAL app.bypass_rls = 'on' inside a\n38\t *   --     transaction; the setting is reset on COMMIT/ROLLBACK so it\n39\t *   --     cannot leak into pooled connections.\n40\t *   current_setting('app.bypass_rls', true) = 'on'\n41\t *\n42\t *   OR\n43\t *\n44\t *   -- (2) Tenant context active and matches the row's tenant.\n45\t *   --     NULLIF turns the empty string (when the GUC is unset) into NULL,\n46\t *   --     which makes the cast safe and forces the comparison to UNKNOWN —\n47\t *   --     so no rows are exposed by accident.\n48\t *   \"<tenant_col>\" = NULLIF(current_setting('app.current_tenant', true), '')::uuid\n49\t * )\n50\t * ```\n51\t *\n52\t * Behaviour matrix:\n53\t *\n54\t * | bypass GUC | tenant GUC      | Result                                |\n55\t * | ---------- | --------------- | ------------------------------------- |\n56\t * | unset      | unset           | No rows visible (deny by default)     |\n57\t * | unset      | UUID            | Only rows where tenantId matches      |\n58\t * | 'on'       | unset           | All rows visible (admin/job mode)     |\n59\t * | 'on'       | UUID            | All rows visible (bypass wins)        |\n60\t *\n61\t * # Discovery semantics\n62\t *\n63\t * The helper introspects `information_schema.columns` for the *current*\n64\t * schema and finds every base table that contains a column whose name is in\n65\t * `tenantIdColumns`. Each match gets RLS enabled, FORCED (so even the table\n66\t * owner is subject to the policy), and a `tenant_isolation_policy` created.\n67\t *\n68\t * Tables in `excludeTables` are skipped — typically outbox, audit logs, and\n69\t * any deliberately cross-tenant infrastructure tables.\n70\t *\n71\t * # Why FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY?\n72\t *\n73\t * Without FORCE, the table owner bypasses all policies — and the application\n74\t * connects as the table owner (`aquaculture` user per init-schemas.sql). FORCE\n75\t * removes that escape hatch and is therefore mandatory for defence-in-depth.\n76\t *\n77\t * @example\n78\t * ```ts\n79\t * import { applyTenantRlsToSchema } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/rls';\n80\t *\n81\t * export class EnableRowLevelSecurity1781000000000 implements MigrationInterface {\n82\t *   public async up(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n83\t *     await applyTenantRlsToSchema(qr, {\n84\t *       excludeTables: ['billing_outbox', 'audit_logs'],\n85\t *     });\n86\t *   }\n87\t *\n88\t *   public async down(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n89\t *     await removeTenantRlsFromSchema(qr);\n90\t *   }\n91\t * }\n92\t * ```\n93\t */\n94\t\n95\t/** Allowed identifier pattern — letters, digits, underscores, must not start with a digit. */\n96\tconst SAFE_IDENTIFIER_REGEX = /^[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_]*$/;\n97\tconst TENANT_SCHEMA_REGEX = /^tenant_[a-f0-9]{16}$/;\n98\tconst DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV = 'DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY';\n99\tconst SQL_ALTER_TABLE = ['ALTER', 'TABLE'].join(' ');\n100\tconst SQL_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY = ['ROW', 'LEVEL', 'SECURITY'].join(' ');\n101\tconst SQL_CREATE_POLICY = ['CREATE', 'POLICY'].join(' ');\n102\t\n103\t/** Default tenant column names to discover (camelCase + snake_case). */\n104\tconst DEFAULT_TENANT_ID_COLUMNS = ['tenantId', 'tenant_id'] as const;\n105\t\n106\t/**\n107\t * Tables whose rows are IDENTITY PRIMITIVES — queried during the pre-\n108\t * authentication discovery phase BEFORE tenant context can be established.\n109\t *\n110\t * Applying `tenant_isolation_policy` to these tables is a category error:\n111\t *\n112\t *   - `users` is searched by email/token at login, password reset,\n113\t *     invitation acceptance. At query time there is NO `app.current_tenant`\n114\t *     GUC set (the tenant is DETERMINED by the user row). The policy's\n115\t *     USING clause `tenantId = current_tenant` evaluates to UNKNOWN and\n116\t *     returns 0 rows, breaking login for every tenant.\n117\t *\n118\t *   - `tenants` is searched by slug/domain during login UX (multi-tenant\n119\t *     subdomain routing, tenant status checks). Same pre-auth cross-tenant\n120\t *     access pattern.\n121\t *\n122\t *   - SUPER_ADMIN users by design have `tenantId = NULL`, which can never\n123\t *     satisfy `tenantId = <any uuid>` — so RLS structurally hides every\n124\t *     platform administrator from the login flow.\n125\t *\n126\t * Defense-in-depth on these tables is enforced OUT-OF-BAND:\n127\t *   1. Schema-role isolation (`auth_service` PG role is the only client).\n128\t *   2. Application-layer explicit `WHERE tenantId = ?` on all post-auth\n129\t *      tenant-scoped queries.\n130\t *   3. JWT-authenticated handlers with TenantGuard enforce tenant context.\n131\t *\n132\t * The helper auto-skips these table names in any schema it sweeps. This\n133\t * lives at the helper layer (not only at the caller's `excludeTables`)\n134\t * because the invariant is platform-wide: no identity primitive in ANY\n135\t * schema may be RLS-protected by tenant_isolation_policy. Tier-1 \"make\n136\t * impossible\" — a new service that accidentally uses `autoApply: true`\n137\t * against an auth-like schema cannot re-introduce the 2026-04-21 login\n138\t * outage. Logged at WARN so the skip is greppable in deploy audits.\n139\t *\n140\t * To install per-tenant isolation on a table that HAPPENS to share one of\n141\t * these names (highly unusual), the caller must pass an explicit\n142\t * `allowIdentityTables: true` option (not yet exposed — add with care\n143\t * and an ADR if a legitimate case ever arises).\n144\t */\n145\texport const DEFAULT_IDENTITY_TABLES = ['users', 'tenants'] as const;\n146\t\n147\t/** The single, canonical policy name. Stable across migrations so DROP IF EXISTS works. */\n148\texport const TENANT_ISOLATION_POLICY_NAME = 'tenant_isolation_policy';\n149\t\n150\t/** Session GUC keys. Public so callers (BypassRlsService, RlsConnectionBootstrap) can reuse. */"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e146bd02-c5f5-4f1e-952e-b26450ca601a","uuid":"2d8f7a03-8c35-4444-8b1a-7753b3ef1ba0","timestamp":"2026-10-06T10:05:14.386Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-consensus-arbiter-6a58206c561b/libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts","content":"import { Logger } from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { QueryRunner } from 'typeorm';\n\n/**\n * applyTenantRlsToSchema\n * ============================================================================\n *\n * Bulk Row-Level Security (RLS) installer for tenant-scoped tables, designed\n * to be invoked from a TypeORM `MigrationInterface.up()`.\n *\n * # Why a helper instead of per-service hand-written SQL?\n *\n * 1. **Single source of truth** for the RLS predicate. The predicate is\n *    SECURITY-CRITICAL — a typo silently allows cross-tenant reads.\n * 2. **Bug fix**: the previous farm-service migration used\n *    `COALESCE(current_setting('app.current_tenant', true), '')::uuid` which\n *    throws `invalid input syntax for type uuid: \"\"` whenever the GUC is\n *    unset. We use `NULLIF(...)::uuid` so an unset GUC becomes NULL and the\n *    comparison cleanly evaluates to UNKNOWN (no cast error, no rows leaked).\n * 3. **Column-name flexibility**: the platform mixes naming conventions —\n *    `farm-service.BaseEntity` uses `tenant_id` (snake_case), while\n *    `auth-service.User` uses `\"tenantId\"` (camelCase quoted). Per-table\n *    discovery handles both.\n * 4. **Bypass clause** baked in: admin-api-service, background workers, and\n *    cross-tenant analytics queries set `app.bypass_rls = 'on'` for the\n *    duration of a transaction. The policy honours this without needing a\n *    separate DB role (which would be operationally heavier).\n * 5. **Idempotent**: safe to re-run after partial failures or in dev/staging\n *    where the schema may already have policies installed (DROP IF EXISTS +\n *    CREATE).\n *\n * # The RLS predicate (security-critical — read carefully)\n *\n * ```sql\n * USING (\n *   -- (1) Explicit bypass for SUPER_ADMIN/background-job contexts.\n *   --     The caller must SET LOCAL app.bypass_rls = 'on' inside a\n *   --     transaction; the setting is reset on COMMIT/ROLLBACK so it\n *   --     cannot leak into pooled connections.\n *   current_setting('app.bypass_rls', true) = 'on'\n *\n *   OR\n *\n *   -- (2) Tenant context active and matches the row's tenant.\n *   --     NULLIF turns the empty string (when the GUC is unset) into NULL,\n *   --     which makes the cast safe and forces the comparison to UNKNOWN —\n *   --     so no rows are exposed by accident.\n *   \"<tenant_col>\" = NULLIF(current_setting('app.current_tenant', true), '')::uuid\n * )\n * ```\n *\n * Behaviour matrix:\n *\n * | bypass GUC | tenant GUC      | Result                                |\n * | ---------- | --------------- | ------------------------------------- |\n * | unset      | unset           | No rows visible (deny by default)     |\n * | unset      | UUID            | Only rows where tenantId matches      |\n * | 'on'       | unset           | All rows visible (admin/job mode)     |\n * | 'on'       | UUID            | All rows visible (bypass wins)        |\n *\n * # Discovery semantics\n *\n * The helper introspects `information_schema.columns` for the *current*\n * schema and finds every base table that contains a column whose name is in\n * `tenantIdColumns`. Each match gets RLS enabled, FORCED (so even the table\n * owner is subject to the policy), and a `tenant_isolation_policy` created.\n *\n * Tables in `excludeTables` are skipped — typically outbox, audit logs, and\n * any deliberately cross-tenant infrastructure tables.\n *\n * # Why FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY?\n *\n * Without FORCE, the table owner bypasses all policies — and the application\n * connects as the table owner (`aquaculture` user per init-schemas.sql). FORCE\n * removes that escape hatch and is therefore mandatory for defence-in-depth.\n *\n * @example\n * ```ts\n * import { applyTenantRlsToSchema } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/rls';\n *\n * export class EnableRowLevelSecurity1781000000000 implements MigrationInterface {\n *   public async up(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n *     await applyTenantRlsToSchema(qr, {\n *       excludeTables: ['billing_outbox', 'audit_logs'],\n *     });\n *   }\n *\n *   public async down(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n *     await removeTenantRlsFromSchema(qr);\n *   }\n * }\n * ```\n */\n\n/** Allowed identifier pattern — letters, digits, underscores, must not start with a digit. */\nconst SAFE_IDENTIFIER_REGEX = /^[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_]*$/;\nconst TENANT_SCHEMA_REGEX = /^tenant_[a-f0-9]{16}$/;\nconst DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV = 'DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY';\nconst SQL_ALTER_TABLE = ['ALTER', 'TABLE'].join(' ');\nconst SQL_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY = ['ROW', 'LEVEL', 'SECURITY'].join(' ');\nconst SQL_CREATE_POLICY = ['CREATE', 'POLICY'].join(' ');\n\n/** Default tenant column names to discover (camelCase + snake_case). */\nconst DEFAULT_TENANT_ID_COLUMNS = ['tenantId', 'tenant_id'] as const;\n\n/**\n * Tables whose rows are IDENTITY PRIMITIVES — queried during the pre-\n * authentication discovery phase BEFORE tenant context can be established.\n *\n * Applying `tenant_isolation_policy` to these tables is a category error:\n *\n *   - `users` is searched by email/token at login, password reset,\n *     invitation acceptance. At query time there is NO `app.current_tenant`\n *     GUC set (the tenant is DETERMINED by the user row). The policy's\n *     USING clause `tenantId = current_tenant` evaluates to UNKNOWN and\n *     returns 0 rows, breaking login for every tenant.\n *\n *   - `tenants` is searched by slug/domain during login UX (multi-tenant\n *     subdomain routing, tenant status checks). Same pre-auth cross-tenant\n *     access pattern.\n *\n *   - SUPER_ADMIN users by design have `tenantId = NULL`, which can never\n *     satisfy `tenantId = <any uuid>` — so RLS structurally hides every\n *     platform administrator from the login flow.\n *\n * Defense-in-depth on these tables is enforced OUT-OF-BAND:\n *   1. Schema-role isolation (`auth_service` PG role is the only client).\n *   2. Application-layer explicit `WHERE tenantId = ?` on all post-auth\n *      tenant-scoped queries.\n *   3. JWT-authenticated handlers with TenantGuard enforce tenant context.\n *\n * The helper auto-skips these table names in any schema it sweeps. This\n * lives at the helper layer (not only at the caller's `excludeTables`)\n * because the invariant is platform-wide: no identity primitive in ANY\n * schema may be RLS-protected by tenant_isolation_policy. Tier-1 \"make\n * impossible\" — a new service that accidentally uses `autoApply: true`\n * against an auth-like schema cannot re-introduce the 2026-04-21 login\n * outage. Logged at WARN so the skip is greppable in deploy audits.\n *\n * To install per-tenant isolation on a table that HAPPENS to share one of\n * these names (highly unusual), the caller must pass an explicit\n * `allowIdentityTables: true` option (not yet exposed — add with care\n * and an ADR if a legitimate case ever arises).\n */\nexport const DEFAULT_IDENTITY_TABLES = ['users', 'tenants'] as const;\n\n/** The single, canonical policy name. Stable across migrations so DROP IF EXISTS works. */\nexport const TENANT_ISOLATION_POLICY_NAME = 'tenant_isolation_policy';\n\n/** Session GUC keys. Public so callers (BypassRlsService, RlsConnectionBootstrap) can reuse. */","numLines":150,"startLine":1,"totalLines":623}}}

\"judge:tenant-scoping-adapter:finding:3d966bad0982196210d2a8b4949cc514c419efab33d26a516c7cb4a161493a41\",\n      \"tool_id\": \"tenant-scoping-adapter\",\n      \"run_id\": \"4c1384cd-7dfe-4ff2-a4d9-8e32e1b64589\",\n      \"verdict\": \"false_positive\",\n      \"confidence\": 0.86,\n      \"judge_count\": 3,\n      \"judges\": [\"aria-evidence-judge\", \"aria-adversarial-judge\", \"aria-consensus-arbiter\"],\n      \"mean_confidence\": 0.78,\n      \"prior_verdicts_survive\": true,\n      \"anchor_grade_blocked_by\": \"mean_confidence_0.78_below_0.80\",\n      \"rationale\": \"Independent judgment, built from the repository rather than from the prior pair. (1) What the rule actually asserts: tenant-scoping-adapter.ts:265 mints this id only after the gate at :254 — the call must survive allowlist, cross-tenant comment, tenant-manager receiver, migration path, scope-has-tenant and call-has-tenant-predicate checks (:230), and the SQL must name a table whose entity declares a tenantId field. So the rule claims a tenant-owned raw statement runs in a tenant-aware scope with no tenant restriction. (2) The restriction exists, one frame above the call. Each statement runs inside withTenantQueryRunner (handler:169-211): search_path is pinned transaction-locally to the tenant schema ahead of the source schema (:177-:185), then the RLS context is bound and PROVEN before the closure body executes (:208-:211) — bindTenantRlsContext sets app.current_tenant (tenant-transaction.ts:182), forces app.bypass_rls off (:185), reads both back, and throws TenantContextError on any mismatch (:202). A silently unbound GUC therefore cannot reach the SQL. (3) The database side is armed, in both schema branches. messaging declares tenantRls hardening (schema-registry.ts:238 -> :153); the per-tenant provisioner installs it in every tenant_<uuid> schema (tenant-schema-provisioner.ts:320) and the source schema is armed by its own baseline (Baseline.ts:82). The installer ENABLEs and FORCEs row level security (apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:527, :531) and creates tenant_isolation_policy FOR ALL with USING reused as WITH CHECK (:547), so reads and the destructive statements at handler:585, :611, :633, :670 are fenced by the row's tenant column, not by the SQL's predicates. (4) The tables the closure touches are discoverable by that installer: messages (message.entity.ts:51), message_attachments (message-attachment.entity.ts:32), message_analysis (message-analysis.entity.ts:73), message_entity_references (message-entity-reference.entity.ts:50) each declare a non-nullable tenantId, and channel_members is shown to carry tenantId by the predicate-bearing lookup at handler:243. Because the rule only fires when the SQL names a table whose entity declares tenantId, every site it can flag is by construction a table the installer arms — that is the structural reason this rule produces this class of miss on NATS handlers. (5) Why the adapter cannot see any of it: the scope window is the innermost function-like node (tenant-scoping-adapter.ts:221, :472), which here is the arrow closure opened at the anchor line, so the wrapper that establishes tenancy is outside the text the rule reads. The missing tenantId predicate is an absent second layer — the repository states that ordering itself at handler:236 — not an absent isolation boundary, so the high-severity actionable claim does not hold. (6) Anchor integrity, independently confirmed: at the state I read, line 530 is the statement that opens withTenantQueryRunner, whose method name is neither a repository nor a raw-query method, so the adapter exits before any finding can be minted there; the first raw statement now begins at :534, four lines below, matching the four-line comment block above the anchor. The sole evidence ref the finding carries therefore no longer resolves to a flagged call site. (7) Residual uncertainty behind the 0.86: I verified installer code and migration wiring, not a live database; the installer skips TimescaleDB columnstore hypertables in tenant schemas, and withTenantQueryRunner binds the RLS context without the current_schema() read-back that the fuller assertion in tenant-transaction.ts performs — a tenant schema missing a table would fall through to the source schema. That fall-through target is itself armed (Baseline.ts:82), and rows inside a tenant schema belong to that tenant, so neither path yields cross-tenant exposure; both rest on static reading. (8) Consequence I will not paper over: the two prior confidences average 0.74 and adding mine lifts the three-judge mean only to 0.78, under the 0.80 bar. The verdict is false_positive and I back it, yet this agreement should reach operator adjudication rather than anchor-grade suppression of the rule class.\",\n      \"evidence_refs\": [\n \"why_it_matters\": \"A settled verdict at anchor grade suppresses this finding class, can quarantine the rule, and scores the judges. A third voice that only echoes the first two converts two opinions into an institution without adding a single new measurement.\",\n      \"what_breaks_if_skipped\": \"Ratifying false_positive on a tenant-isolation rule without reading the row-level-security substrate would retire a security rule on the strength of a prose argument.
